# Binding and the Exact Asymmetry Bridge

## ATOM3D: contact, grammar and signed decoding — 14 September 2026

A3D41-T18-CONTACT-R2 uses SLC-GEN3-R3; A3D41-RXT-R3 supplies the joint native successor. Ordinary contact retains four minima and 113,664 agreeing readouts. Li-6 grammar retains both selected covers and every tie across six placements and 128 rho settings. Two Write responses and a signed N01 bit recover all 192 tested configurations. The test result suggests strong contact with the concept. The separate distinct-selector and physical-coefficient/MeV work remain owner-paused.

[Current derivations, code and results](../../research/atom3d/README.md).

## Retained source-era derivation and results

The following development retains its original experimental context and revision fields. Historical engine selections and campaign status in this source-era account are superseded by the dated current section above.


## Opening question

> What exact nuclear-binding structure survives the failed direct
> source-to-rest-mass identity, how does the neutron-excess asymmetry term
> emerge one algebraic step at a time, and which additional geometric and
> readout operators are still required?

## Conceptual abstract

The binding route begins with a failure that reveals a difference of channels.
CR239 applies one conversion to the native coupling source and finds that the
result does not reproduce the rest-mass data. The residual aligns strongly
with neutron excess. CR240 therefore introduces two typed constructions:

\[
Q_{\rm mass}=4A\kappa,
\qquad
Q_{\rm sub}=8Z\kappa+\frac{N-Z}{8}.
\]

Their difference is not guessed from a binding curve. It follows exactly:

\[
\Delta Q
=Q_{\rm mass}-Q_{\rm sub}
=(N-Z)\frac{7093}{192}.
\]

Squaring that gap and normalizing by \(Q_{\rm mass}\) produces

\[
\frac{(\Delta Q)^2}{Q_{\rm mass}}
=
\frac{(N-Z)^2}{A}
\frac{50310649}{1366464}.
\]

The familiar neutron-asymmetry shape \((N-Z)^2/A\) is therefore the exact
normalized square of a typed rest-source/coupling-source mismatch. CR245
checks the identity under exact rational arithmetic across its 71-row roster.

That identity is one binding component, not the complete operator. A separate
linear connection-fee attempt misses the binding curvature by tens of MeV.
The registered CR249 A-kernel instead restores volume, surface, Coulomb,
asymmetry and pairing shapes. CR250 preserves a monotonic but nonseparating
intermediate route; CR251 removes coefficient degeneracy in its principal
lane and distinguishes the closed-ledger \(1/(S\mathcal L)\) asymmetry scale
from the bare-cycle \(1/(SR^2)\) alternative. CR274 is a later historical
fitted readout with family gates. Current binding authority has since moved
to B5T, so the Volume II records remain the exact source and derivation
history rather than an automatic statement of the present binding frontier.

## 1. Binding starts after source typing

The operation order is

\[
(Z,N)
\longrightarrow
Q_{\rm mass},Q_{\rm sub}
\longrightarrow
\Delta Q
\longrightarrow
\frac{(\Delta Q)^2}{Q_{\rm mass}}
\longrightarrow
\mathcal G_{\rm bind}
\longrightarrow
\mathcal R_{\rm nuclear}.
\]

Here:

- \(Q_{\rm mass}\) is a rest-source channel;
- \(Q_{\rm sub}\) is a substrate-coupling source;
- \(\Delta Q\) is their signed mismatch;
- the normalized square is the asymmetry-shape input;
- \(\mathcal G_{\rm bind}\) contains the nonlinear nuclear geometry; and
- \(\mathcal R_{\rm nuclear}\) is the downstream comparison or fitted
  readout.

The forbidden shortcut is

\[
Q_{\rm sub}
\longrightarrow
m_{\rm measured}
\longrightarrow
B_{\rm nucleus}
\]

with no type correction or nonlinear geometry. CR239 and the connection-fee
failure each reject one part of that shortcut.

## 2. Definitions, domains and units

| Symbol or object | Definition | Domain or unit | Boundary |
|---|---|---|---|
| \(Z\) | proton number | nonnegative integer | Element-family index, not a unique isotope. |
| \(N\) | neutron number | nonnegative integer | This derivation uses the declared nuclear row. |
| \(A\) | \(Z+N\) | nucleon count | Not the accumulation field \(A(r)\). |
| \(\kappa\) | \(7117/768\) | exact source coefficient | Fixed before binding comparison. |
| \(Q_{\rm mass}\) | \(4A\kappa\) | source units | Rest-source construction. |
| \(Q_{\rm sub}\) | \(8Z\kappa+(N-Z)/8\) | source units | Coupling-source construction. |
| \(\Delta Q\) | \(Q_{\rm mass}-Q_{\rm sub}\) | source units | Signed gap; zero for \(N=Z\). |
| \(X_A\) | \((\Delta Q)^2/Q_{\rm mass}\) | source units | Exact asymmetry-shape column, not complete binding energy. |
| \(\rho_A\) | \(A^{1/3}\) | dimensionless nuclear-size coordinate in the A-kernel | Geometric readout coordinate. |
| \(\delta_{\rm pair}\) | source-defined parity/pairing column | finite signed class | One model component. |
| \(B_u\) | source nuclear binding/mass-defect comparator | u or MeV after declared conversion | External/readout target, not a substrate primitive. |
| \(c_V,c_S,c_C,c_A,c_P\) | A-kernel coefficients | source-fit units | Fitted unless a separate source fixes a typed candidate. |
| \(\mathcal L\) | closed-ledger value | \(162\) | Distinct from \(R^2=144\). |

The lift coefficients \(5/48\), \(17/192\) and the unequal-pair forms from
SAMA-D000028 are row-class surface operators. They are not interchangeable
with \(Q_{\rm mass}\), \(Q_{\rm sub}\) or \(X_A\). SAMA-C000147-R001,
SAMA-C000149-R001 and SAMA-C000151-R001 retain that firewall.

## 3. Why the direct bridge had to deviate

CR239 tests

\[
m_i=m_g=\mu_QQ
\]

with one carbon-12 anchor. The anchor closes, but the Lane-A RMS residual is
\(0.189480\), compared with \(0.00202\) for the locked mass-number control.
The direct channel is therefore about ninety times worse than the control.

The residual carries a clear neutron-excess direction:

\[
\rho_S(\epsilon,N-Z)=+0.984,
\]

with \(N=Z\) subset RMS \(0.0023\) and \(N\ne Z\) subset RMS \(0.2152\).
The source artifact records FAIL_CR239_DIRECT_BRIDGE.

This result does two things at once:

1. it rejects \(Q_{\rm sub}\) as a direct measured-rest-mass channel; and
2. it retains the \(Q_{\rm sub}\) structure as a coupling source whose
   difference from a rest-source channel is organized by \(N-Z\).

SAMA-C000131-R001 and SAMA-C000155-R001 carry that distinction. Erasing the
failed run would erase the reason the two-channel derivation exists.

## 4. The typed correction before asymmetry

CR240 supplies

\[
Q_{\rm mass}=4A\kappa.
\]

With the carbon-12 bridge, its mass-number readout is \(A\,{\rm u}\). On 49
non-anchor Lane-A rows the source records RMS fractional residual
\(0.002024\), while the residual retains volume, surface, Coulomb and
asymmetry structure.

The coupling channel remains

\[
Q_{\rm sub}=8Z\kappa+\frac{N-Z}{8}.
\]

For \(N=Z\), the channels meet:

\[
Q_{\rm mass}
=4(2Z)\kappa
=8Z\kappa
=Q_{\rm sub}.
\]

For neutron excess, they separate. CR241 then challenges the candidate
extension on 20 unique holdout rows. It records holdout RMS
\(0.000686400048095\), anchor stability and intact control structure, but its
candidate scan contains 30 nucleon-window hits and six splitting-window hits.
Neither declared uniqueness threshold closes. The source artifact therefore
records FAIL_CR241_CANDIDATE_ARTIFACT while leaving the CR240 channel usable.

This is SAMA-C000168-R001's precise status: structure retained, uniqueness
unpromoted.

## 5. Direct-bridge deviation and exact asymmetry correction

This section carries the derivation at atomic granularity.

### 5.1 Start from the two typed channels

\[
Q_{\rm mass}=4A\kappa
\]

and

\[
Q_{\rm sub}=8Z\kappa+\frac{N-Z}{8}.
\]

Use

\[
A=Z+N.
\]

### 5.2 Substitute \(A=Z+N\)

\[
\begin{aligned}
\Delta Q
&=Q_{\rm mass}-Q_{\rm sub}\\
&=4A\kappa
 -\left(8Z\kappa+\frac{N-Z}{8}\right)\\
&=4(Z+N)\kappa-8Z\kappa-\frac{N-Z}{8}.
\end{aligned}
\]

The parentheses matter: the one-eighth term belongs to the coupling channel
and is subtracted with it.

### 5.3 Expand the rest-source term

\[
4(Z+N)\kappa=4Z\kappa+4N\kappa.
\]

Hence

\[
\Delta Q
=4Z\kappa+4N\kappa-8Z\kappa-\frac{N-Z}{8}.
\]

### 5.4 Collect the \(\kappa\) terms

\[
4Z\kappa-8Z\kappa=-4Z\kappa,
\]

so

\[
\Delta Q
=4N\kappa-4Z\kappa-\frac{N-Z}{8}.
\]

Factor \(4\kappa\):

\[
4N\kappa-4Z\kappa
=4\kappa(N-Z).
\]

Therefore

\[
\Delta Q
=4\kappa(N-Z)-\frac{N-Z}{8}.
\]

### 5.5 Factor neutron excess

Both terms contain \(N-Z\):

\[
\Delta Q
=(N-Z)\left(4\kappa-\frac18\right).
\]

This is the conceptual turning point. Nuclear imbalance is not inserted as an
independent empirical column; it appears because the balanced \(Z\)-dependent
parts cancel between the two source channels.

### 5.6 Reduce the per-excess gap exactly

Since

\[
\kappa=\frac{7117}{768},
\]

\[
4\kappa
=4\frac{7117}{768}
=\frac{7117}{192}.
\]

Put \(1/8\) over the same denominator:

\[
\frac18=\frac{24}{192}.
\]

Subtract:

\[
4\kappa-\frac18
=\frac{7117}{192}-\frac{24}{192}
=\frac{7117-24}{192}
=\frac{7093}{192}.
\]

Thus

\[
\boxed{
\Delta Q=(N-Z)\frac{7093}{192}.}
\]

This is the complete SAMA-C000169-R001 derivation.

### 5.7 Square the exact gap

\[
\begin{aligned}
(\Delta Q)^2
&=\left((N-Z)\frac{7093}{192}\right)^2\\
&=(N-Z)^2\frac{7093^2}{192^2}.
\end{aligned}
\]

The square removes the sign of the channel mismatch while retaining its
magnitude and makes the penalty symmetric under \(N-Z\mapsto Z-N\) at the
shape level.

### 5.8 Rewrite the normalizer

\[
Q_{\rm mass}=4A\kappa.
\]

Insert \(\kappa=7117/768\):

\[
\begin{aligned}
Q_{\rm mass}
&=4A\frac{7117}{768}\\
&=A\frac{7117}{192}.
\end{aligned}
\]

### 5.9 Divide and cancel

\[
\begin{aligned}
\frac{(\Delta Q)^2}{Q_{\rm mass}}
&=
\frac{(N-Z)^2\,7093^2/192^2}
     {A\,7117/192}\\
&=
(N-Z)^2\frac{7093^2}{192^2}
\frac{192}{A\,7117}.
\end{aligned}
\]

Cancel one factor of \(192\):

\[
\frac{192}{192^2}=\frac1{192}.
\]

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
\frac{(\Delta Q)^2}{Q_{\rm mass}}
=
\frac{(N-Z)^2}{A}
\frac{7093^2}{192\cdot7117}.}
\]

### 5.10 Reduce the coefficient

\[
7093^2=50310649
\]

and

\[
192\cdot7117=1366464.
\]

Hence

\[
\boxed{
\frac{(\Delta Q)^2}{Q_{\rm mass}}
=
\frac{(N-Z)^2}{A}
\frac{50310649}{1366464}.}
\]

Numerically,

\[
\frac{50310649}{1366464}
\approx36.8181298593.
\]

This completes SAMA-C000170-R001. The rational coefficient must be retained
exactly in computation before decimal display.

### 5.11 Balanced and single-excess checks

If \(N=Z\), then

\[
\Delta Q=0
\]

and

\[
\frac{(\Delta Q)^2}{Q_{\rm mass}}=0.
\]

For carbon-13, \(Z=6,N=7,A=13\), so

\[
\Delta Q=\frac{7093}{192},
\]

\[
Q_{\rm mass}=13\frac{7117}{192}
=\frac{92521}{192},
\]

and

\[
\frac{(\Delta Q)^2}{Q_{\rm mass}}
=\frac1{13}
\frac{50310649}{1366464}.
\]

These endpoint checks use no fitted binding coefficient.

### 5.12 CR245 execution and boundary

CR245 checks the identity on 71/71 rows with exact rational arithmetic.
Changing \(R\) to 10, 11 or 13 breaks all 58 nonzero-asymmetry rows for each
control; the 13 \(N=Z\) rows remain zero on both sides, as they must. Dropping
the asymmetry shape increases the source fit RMS from \(2.78\) to
\(12.47\) MeV, while shuffling the binding target increases it to
\(35.69\) MeV.

The exact export is the normalized-square identity. The same source preserves
that its zero-free typed coefficient substitutions do not recover the fitted
full curve. This is why SAMA-C000171-R001 requires nonlinear geometry beyond
the exact asymmetry component.

## 6. Native binding prerequisites

The registered precursor chain is:

\[
\text{QP:QP040}
\longrightarrow
\text{QP:QP047}
\longrightarrow
\text{QP:QP051}
\longrightarrow
\text{QP:QP052}.
\]

QP:QP040 supplies the native binding-residue operator framing. QP:QP047
selects the nuclear binding/isotope \(A\)-road. QP:QP051 selects
neutron-excess depth. QP:QP052 supplies the numeric \(\Delta N\) and
binding-mass comparison route.

Together they define the question and inputs. They do not close the
calibrated binding operator. In particular, a measured binding value remains
a comparator and cannot be used to select the native row or redefine
\(\kappa\).

The required nonlinear shape roster is

\[
\boxed{
\text{volume}
+\text{surface}
+\text{Coulomb}
+\text{asymmetry}
+\text{pairing}
+\text{gated family structure}.}
\]

The signs and coefficients are applied in a declared operation order; the
roster is not an invitation to add terms after reading residuals.

## 7. A-kernel failure, correction and historical retest

### 7.1 Linear connection-fee failure

SAMA-C000172-R001 preserves a separate source artifact that tests six
zero-fit sums of substrate-atom connection fees on 69 scored nuclei. Its best
rule is the per-excess-neutron sum, yet it records

\[
\max|\Delta|=89.85\ {\rm MeV}
\]

against the locked ceiling

\[
0.005\ {\rm MeV}.
\]

The candidate roster includes linear volume, proton-neutron pair,
channel-gap, squared-asymmetry and combined alternatives. None reproduces the
curvature. The failure establishes that a nucleus is not bound by a simple
linear count of particle-level connection fees.

Registry firewall: the current key CR:CR249@09a resolves to the A-kernel
artifact below. It does not resolve to this separate connection-fee artifact.
No second qualified SAMA test key is currently registered, so the
connection-fee source is carried through SAMA-C000172-R001 and is not given an
invented test-index row.

### 7.2 Registered A-kernel geometry

The registered CR:CR249@09a defines

\[
\rho_A=A^{1/3}.
\]

From that coordinate, the five shapes are

\[
V_A=\rho_A^3=A,
\]

\[
S_A=\rho_A^2=A^{2/3},
\]

\[
C_A=\frac{Z(Z-1)}{\rho_A}
=\frac{Z(Z-1)}{A^{1/3}},
\]

\[
X_A=\frac{(\Delta Q)^2}{Q_{\rm mass}},
\]

and the declared pairing column \(\delta_{\rm pair}\).

The fitted model is

\[
B_{\rm A\mbox{-}kernel}
=c_VA
-c_SA^{2/3}
-c_C\frac{Z(Z-1)}{A^{1/3}}
-c_AX_A
+c_P\delta_{\rm pair}.
\]

On the source split, the free-shape fit records

\[
\mathrm{RMS}_{\rm train}=2.7260\ {\rm MeV},
\qquad
R^2_{\rm train}=0.99458,
\]

\[
\mathrm{RMS}_{\rm test}=3.5218\ {\rm MeV},
\qquad
R^2_{\rm test}=0.98502.
\]

The fitted asymmetry coefficient is

\[
c_A^{\rm fit}=7.7504\times10^{-4}\ {\rm u},
\]

while the typed closed-ledger candidate is

\[
c_A^{\rm typed}
=\frac1{S\mathcal L}
=\frac1{8\cdot162}
=\frac1{1296}
\approx7.71605\times10^{-4}\ {\rm u}.
\]

Their relative difference is \(0.44\%\), the first within-1% typed
coefficient in this source arc.

The sensitivity ledger prevents over-compression:

- removing volume, surface, Coulomb or asymmetry worsens RMS by roughly
  \(7.49\), \(6.17\), \(5.26\) and \(4.54\) times;
- removing pairing worsens RMS by \(1.16\) times, below its declared
  \(1.5\)-times control gate;
- changing surface \(A^{2/3}\) to \(A^{1/2}\) worsens RMS by only
  \(1.14\) times, below its \(1.2\)-times gate;
- changing the Coulomb road from \(A^{-1/3}\) to \(A^{-2/3}\) worsens RMS
  by \(2.06\) times; and
- shuffling the target worsens RMS by \(13.02\) times.

Substituting all three within-5% typed candidates while retaining the other
two fitted coefficients raises RMS to \(21.71\) MeV on train and \(31.91\)
MeV on test. The A-kernel therefore recovers the needed geometry and a close
typed \(c_A\) candidate, but it is not a zero-free complete binding law.
This is SAMA-C000173-R001.

### 7.3 CR250 nonseparation

CR:CR250@09a applies the sealed particle-level lift form to the balanced and
excess positions. Its raw prediction is monotonic in the intended direction,
but the best single rescale still leaves

\[
\mathrm{RMS}=43.4176\ {\rm MeV}.
\]

Its designated wrong controls do not break the rescaled shape: depth,
\(D\), \(\mu_Q\), gap-label and shuffle substitutions remain nonseparating at
the locked gates. The source therefore records BOUNDARY_WC_FAIL. In the
coefficient-refinement lineage summarized by CR251, the corresponding
limitation is parameter degeneracy: the closed-ledger
\(1/(S\mathcal L)\) candidate is not separated from the bare-cycle
\(1/(SR^2)\) alternative by a coupled refit.

The precise export is failure of separation, not failure of monotonicity.

### 7.4 CR251 orthogonalized correction

CR251 removes non-asymmetry columns before comparing the two typed scales.
Let

\[
X_0=
\begin{bmatrix}
A&
-A^{2/3}&
-Z(Z-1)A^{-1/3}&
\delta_{\rm pair}
\end{bmatrix},
\]

and let \(X_A\) be the signed asymmetry column. Form

\[
P_0=X_0(X_0^TX_0)^{-1}X_0^T,
\]

\[
B_\perp=(I-P_0)B_u,
\qquad
X_{A,\perp}=(I-P_0)X_A.
\]

The isolated coefficient is

\[
c_{A,\rm orth}
=\frac{X_{A,\perp}^TB_\perp}
       {X_{A,\perp}^TX_{A,\perp}}.
\]

On the principal \(n=55\) lane,

\[
c_{A,\rm orth}
=7.339\times10^{-4}\ {\rm u},
\qquad
\mathrm{SE}=2.343\times10^{-5}\ {\rm u}.
\]

The closed-ledger candidate

\[
\frac1{S\mathcal L}=\frac1{1296}
\]

lies \(1.61\) standard errors from the estimate, inside the declared 95%
interval. The bare-cycle candidate

\[
\frac1{SR^2}=\frac1{8\cdot144}=\frac1{1152}
\]

lies \(5.73\) standard errors away, outside the declared 99% interval.
Thus the principal orthogonalized lane distinguishes the bounce-aware
closed-ledger scale.

The correction retains its own boundaries:

- the \(|N-Z|\ge20\) stress lane has only 24 rows and places both candidates
  inside its wider interval;
- the isobaric lane has only two pairs for three unknown contrast
  coefficients and is recorded as insufficient data; and
- four wrong controls pass: binding-target shuffle, \(N=Z\) degeneracy,
  a random added feature and asymmetry-column permutation.

SAMA-C000174-R001 therefore records a narrowed asymmetry scale without a
complete binding closure.

### 7.5 Historical CR274 retest/readout

CR:CR274@09a locks the exact CR245 asymmetry coefficient

\[
\frac{50310649}{1366464}
\approx36.81813
\]

inside a fitted Model K and applies four recorded family operators:

| Operator | Recorded role | Coefficient |
|---|---|---:|
| \(op_{82pre}\) | pre-82 family | \(-0.7273\) |
| \(op_{3d\_odd}\) | odd 3d family | \(+6.9654\) |
| \(op_{dm\_sat}\) | doubly-magic saturation | \(+7.1800\) |
| \(op_{ms\_fill}\) | mid-shell fill | \(-0.4531\) |

The source benchmark changes from

\[
\mathrm{RMS}_{\rm base}=3.9102\ {\rm MeV}
\]

to

\[
\mathrm{RMS}_{\rm gated}=2.7160\ {\rm MeV},
\]

with

\[
50/55
\]

rows within \(5\) MeV. Five source-listed outliers remain. The coefficients
and gate predicates are fitted nuclear-readout parameters. They are not new
substrate primitives. SAMA-C000175-R001 preserves CR274 as historical
readout evidence.

## 8. Current binding authority versus Volume II history

The current live binding authority is
[SAM_LIVE/06_BINDING_CURRENT.md](https://github.com/iwtbotiwtwot/SAM_Research_Project/blob/0952ff63364f9406f389e63f4fdf13893255e828/SAM_LIVE/06_BINDING_CURRENT.md).
It identifies B5T as the current completed representation/residual test and
records the authorized classification:

**The test result suggests strong contact with the concept.**

B5T installs an informational 3D tensor assignment and a leakage-safe held
chain. It does not install a physical tensor, nuclear geometry,
isotope-local tensor, \(A/q_A\) conversion, SI-energy law, Higgs law or final
binding formula.

Therefore:

- CR245 remains the exact Volume II asymmetry derivation;
- CR249–CR251 remain the A-kernel geometry and coefficient-control history;
- CR274 remains a historical fitted nuclear readout;
- B5T controls current binding status; and
- neither B5T nor current ATOM3D work retroactively turns CR274's fitted
  coefficients into substrate primitives.

This is a current-versus-historical boundary, not a competition between
results.

## 9. Established result and exact open work

The source chain establishes:

1. The direct coupling-source/rest-mass identity fails and its residual
   isolates neutron excess as the correction direction.
2. \(Q_{\rm mass}\) and \(Q_{\rm sub}\) are separately typed, and the CR240
   channel remains usable despite CR241's failed uniqueness promotion.
3. Their exact gap is \((N-Z)7093/192\).
4. The normalized square of that gap is exactly
   \((N-Z)^2/A\) times \(50310649/1366464\).
5. A linear connection-fee sum does not recover nuclear binding curvature.
6. The A-kernel supplies volume, surface, Coulomb, asymmetry and pairing
   shapes and locates \(1/(S\mathcal L)\) as the close typed asymmetry scale.
7. CR250 preserves nonseparation; CR251 corrects it on the principal
   orthogonalized lane while retaining low-power and isobaric boundaries.
8. CR274 supplies a historical gated readout, not current binding authority.

The exact open work is to derive the complete nonlinear binding operator from
the connector/carrier and current tensor geometry, including shared origins
for volume, surface, Coulomb, pairing and family gates, without changing the
exact asymmetry bridge.

All proposal crosswalk rows in this chapter carry
\(result\_classification=\mathrm{null}\). The exact current B5T
classification above is quoted from live authority, not assigned by this
document.

## 10. Forward handoff

SAMA-P000003 receives the full matter-side derivation. SAMA-P000006 receives
the finite-particle, binding and computation boundary. Volume III may consume
the exact rationals

\[
\kappa=\frac{7117}{768},
\qquad
\Delta Q=(N-Z)\frac{7093}{192},
\qquad
d_{\rm asym}=\frac{50310649}{1366464},
\]

but it must carry the source types and cannot infer a final binding operator
from those three values alone.

# Carrier/Container Return to Accumulation

## ATOM3D: contact, grammar and signed decoding — 14 September 2026

A3D41-T18-CONTACT-R2 uses SLC-GEN3-R3; A3D41-RXT-R3 supplies the joint native successor. Ordinary contact retains four minima and 113,664 agreeing readouts. Li-6 grammar retains both selected covers and every tie across six placements and 128 rho settings. Two Write responses and a signed N01 bit recover all 192 tested configurations. The test result suggests strong contact with the concept. The separate distinct-selector and physical-coefficient/MeV work remain owner-paused.

[Current derivations, code and results](../../research/atom3d/README.md).

## Retained source-era derivation and results

The following development retains its original experimental context and revision fields. Historical engine selections and campaign status in this source-era account are superseded by the dated current section above.


## Opening question

> Once matter has closed as a finite material write, how does its influence
> return through carriers and containers to the accumulation field without
> turning matter back into substrate, turning \(q_A\) into measured mass,
> adding non-row operators to row counts, or hiding the source tests that left
> an exact weld or geometry open?

## Conceptual abstract

Volume II begins by separating substrate from matter. It ends by showing that
the separation is not dynamical isolation. An admitted matter row remains
matter while its influence is expressed through source support:

\[
q_A=M_{\rm obs}\left(1+\frac{|q|}{R^2}\right).
\]

That support divides exactly:

\[
T=\frac{q_A}{8},\qquad W=\frac{7q_A}{8},
\qquad T+W=q_A.
\]

\(T\) is unresolved carrier support. \(W\) is retained-write support. Neither
is measured rest mass. Ledger compression then produces the source passed to
the accumulation field

\[
A(r)=\frac{r_s}{r},
\]

after which Volume I supplies force, clock, road and strong-field readouts.
The typed route is therefore

\[
\text{closed matter}
\to q_A
\to (T,W)
\to \text{ledger compression}
\to A(r)
\to \text{readout}.
\]

QP:QP092d checks the conservation step across particle, macro and propagation
rows. LC:LC03 replays the complete matter-to-\(A\) mechanism. LC:LC06 retains
the null-conjugate witness that blocks a scalar-value-to-matter shortcut.

The return has internal addresses. CR:CR281@09a classifies exact
\(h^a d^b\) addresses as OUTPUT, FIXED POINT, TERMINAL CLOSURE or OUT.
Released \(\Theta18\) is output traffic; it is not a seated container.
The values 12, 27 and 81 are fixed points; 162 is terminal closure.

The route also preserves two informative failures. CR:CR282@09a retains A as
a non-row operator and keeps the active ledger at 162, but its axis-self weld
is open. A separate source appeal, which has no permanent exact test key,
supports

\[
B/\mathrm{contact}\to X1\text{ axis fee}\to W9
\]

while leaving \(A\to B/\mathrm{contact}\) and direct \(A\to X1\) open.
CR:CR283@09a retains

\[
80+1=81,\qquad 2\cdot81=162,
\]

but both of its precommitted row-to-face searches return zero valid
geometries. The cardinality contact survives; geometry remains an explicit
open relation.

## 1. Return is relation, not category reversal

Matter admission and matter return are two different gates:

\[
\text{candidate}
\xrightarrow{\mathcal G_{\rm matter}}
\text{admitted material row}
\xrightarrow{\mathcal G_{\rm source}}
q_A.
\]

The first gate decides whether a finite closed write is matter. The second
accepts an already admitted observed-matter readout \(M_{\rm obs}\) and a
typed charge or role coordinate \(q\). SAMA-C000129-R001 records

\[
\mathcal G_{\rm source}(M_{\rm obs},q)
=M_{\rm obs}\left(1+\frac{|q|}{R^2}\right).
\]

Using the source gate as an admission rule would reverse the dependency. An
arbitrary scalar could then be assigned a \(q_A\) value and called matter.
The domain restriction prevents that.

The material source remains on the matter side of the boundary:

\[
\operatorname{type}(m)=\mathrm{MatterRow}
\]

before and after it emits

\[
\operatorname{type}(q_A)=\mathrm{SourceSupport}.
\]

No assignment

\[
\operatorname{type}(m)\leftarrow\mathrm{Substrate}
\]

occurs. SAMA-C000005-R001 is the compact route record for this distinction.

## 2. Ontology, domains and units

| Object | Exact role | Domain or unit | Forbidden collapse |
|---|---|---|---|
| \(m\) | admitted finite matter or composite write | typed material row | substrate object |
| \(M_{\rm obs}\) | observed/admitted matter readout entering source gate | source-declared matter unit | arbitrary scalar admission |
| \(q_A\) | charge-adjusted source support | source-support unit | measured rest mass |
| \(T\) | \(q_A/8\), unresolved tensor-carrier support | source-support unit | matter row or fixed container |
| \(W\) | \(7q_A/8\), retained-write support | source-support unit | independent matter row |
| \(\Theta\) | \(R^2/8=18\), released carrier | carrier role | retained lift or ordinary rest mass |
| \(A(r)\) | \(r_s/r\), compressed accumulation field | dimensionless field readout | particle row or A-conjugation operator |
| A operator | non-row antimatter-conjugation transform | row-to-row operator | accumulation scalar, X1 or a new row |
| X1 | transient activator/axis-fee role | substrate-atom relation | tensor or matter row |
| W8 | octahedral substrate atom | substrate object | material composite |
| W9 | completed relation \(X1+W8=W9\) | relational closure | independent ninth object |
| \(F\) | full-face value \(81\) | fixed-point/container address | eighty-first matter row |
| \(\mathcal L\) | terminal closed ledger \(162\) | terminal address | active-row census |
| closure address | non-row contact completing cardinality | address type | particle-bearing row |
| N100 | source-native computation grammar | 100 typed computation rows | rewrite of a physical table |

The basic constants are

\[
S=8,\qquad R=12,\qquad R^2=144,\qquad D=3,
\]

\[
\Theta=\frac{R^2}{S}=18,\qquad
M=\frac{S-1}{S}R^2=126,
\]

\[
W9=S+1=9,\qquad
F=W9^2=81,\qquad
\mathcal L=2F=162.
\]

The equalities connect addresses and capacities. They do not erase the roles
named in the table.

## 3. Full derivation of the matter-to-accumulation route

### 3.1 Construct source support only after admission

Given admitted \(M_{\rm obs}\) and the source-defined coordinate \(q\),

\[
q_A=M_{\rm obs}
\left(1+\frac{|q|}{R^2}\right).
\]

Because \(R^2=144\),

\[
q_A=M_{\rm obs}
\left(1+\frac{|q|}{144}\right).
\]

This is a source-support surcharge. The multiplication does not redefine
\(M_{\rm obs}\); both fields must remain serialized.

### 3.2 Split released and retained support

The substrate multiplicity is \(S=8\). Define

\[
T=\frac{q_A}{S}
\]

and

\[
W=\frac{S-1}{S}q_A.
\]

Substituting \(S=8\) gives

\[
T=\frac18q_A,
\qquad
W=\frac78q_A.
\]

Conservation follows step by step:

\[
\begin{aligned}
T+W
&=\frac18q_A+\frac78q_A\\
&=\frac{1+7}{8}q_A\\
&=\frac88q_A\\
&=q_A.
\end{aligned}
\]

The split is complementary, not duplicative. No support is created or
discarded by the decomposition.

### 3.3 Relate the split to the closed domain

For the domain itself,

\[
\Theta=\frac18R^2=\frac18\cdot144=18
\]

and

\[
M=\frac78R^2=\frac78\cdot144=126.
\]

Thus

\[
\Theta+M=18+126=144=R^2.
\]

This arithmetic mirrors \(T+W=q_A\), but the domain counts and a particular
source packet are different objects. SAMA-C000128-R001 preserves both the
complementarity and the type distinction.

### 3.4 Compress before updating the field

The carrier and retained-write channels are ledger fields. The next operator
is compression:

\[
\mathcal C_{\rm ledger}(T,W,\text{source address})
\longmapsto r_s.
\]

Only then is the accumulation field evaluated:

\[
A(r)=\frac{r_s}{r}.
\]

This separates the material/source grammar from the spatial readout. A local
force, clock comparison or road operator consumes \(A(r)\) under Volume I
rules. It does not consume raw \(q_A\) as measured rest mass.

### 3.5 A worked charged-source example

Take an admitted row with

\[
M_{\rm obs}=12,\qquad |q|=3.
\]

Then

\[
\begin{aligned}
q_A
&=12\left(1+\frac{3}{144}\right)\\
&=12\left(1+\frac1{48}\right)\\
&=12\cdot\frac{49}{48}\\
&=\frac{49}{4}.
\end{aligned}
\]

The carrier share is

\[
T=\frac18\cdot\frac{49}{4}
=\frac{49}{32},
\]

and the retained write is

\[
W=\frac78\cdot\frac{49}{4}
=\frac{343}{32}.
\]

Their sum is

\[
\frac{49}{32}+\frac{343}{32}
=\frac{392}{32}
=\frac{49}{4}
=q_A.
\]

The example is exact. It does not assign the scalar \(49/4\) to a particle
mass or a container address.

## 4. Deviation, wrong-control, correction and retest chain

### 4.1 Direct \(q_A\)-as-mass deviation

The shortest wrong route is

\[
q_A\longrightarrow m_{\rm measured}.
\]

QP:QP092d directly rejects this use on 24 of 24 rows while retaining carrier
conservation. LC:LC03 also rejects direct \(q_A\)-as-mass among 15 of 15 wrong
controls. The correction is the typed chain

\[
q_A\longrightarrow(T,W)
\longrightarrow\mathcal C_{\rm ledger}
\longrightarrow A(r).
\]

The failed shortcut remains part of the explanation because it identifies
the missing carrier and compression operators.

### 4.2 No-carrier and wrong-fraction controls

Deleting \(T\) yields only

\[
W=\frac78q_A\ne q_A.
\]

Replacing \(1/8\) with \(1/4\) or \(1/16\) also changes the paired retained
share and breaks the locked domain relation. LC:LC03 preserves these wrong
fractions, carrier-to-matter promotion, vector/scalar carrier substitution,
mass-only source and no-compression alternatives among its rejected controls.

### 4.3 Carrier-to-matter promotion control

The native carrier value is 18. Its hypothetical retained lift would be

\[
18\left(1+\frac{18}{144}\right)
=18\left(1+\frac18\right)
=18\cdot\frac98
=\frac{81}{4}.
\]

The resulting \(81/4=20.25\) is not a seated target row. The source rest
construction gives

\[
M_{\rm rest}(\Theta)=18-18=0.
\]

CR:CR281@09a therefore returns OUTPUT for \(\Theta18\), not FIXED POINT.
LC:LC06's null witness independently retains native value 18 and debit 18
while closing to

\[
M_{\rm obs}=q_A=0.
\]

The shared number 18 is insufficient for matter admission.

### 4.4 Raw numeric ordering control

Sorting the values \(6,9,12,18,27,54,81,162\) cannot recover functional role.
For example, 12 is a fixed point while the larger 18 is output. CR:CR281@09a
tests raw numeric ordering, parity-only rules, pure-versus-mixed-power rules,
an unrestricted grade, swapped primitive addresses and inverted role labels.
All six wrong controls break. Exact address and release/seating behavior are
required.

### 4.5 Restoring the retired A-field row

CR:CR282@09a traces the historical A-field-carrier row and preserves its
retirement as a footprint duplicate. Restoring it would change the ledger

\[
162\longrightarrow163.
\]

That is the rejected restoration control. The correction retains the active A
concept as a non-row operator and preserves the closed ledger at 162.

### 4.6 Geometry failure after cardinality closure

The exact contact

\[
80+1=81
\]

does not select coordinates for the 80 rows. CR:CR283@09a therefore performs
two precommitted searches:

- a punctured \(9\times9\) row map; and
- a single-field ten-octet grouping.

Each returns zero valid arrangements. The correction is not to discard the
arithmetic. It splits the result:

\[
\text{cardinality and typed-ledger contact retained},
\]

\[
\text{specific row-to-face geometry open}.
\]

### 4.7 Retest and computation boundary

G:G1@SAM-RESEARCH audits the source-native N100-to-F81 compiler surface. It
finds 100 unique N100 source keys, 72 rows in the exact aggregate-incidence
domain and an 81-site, ten-class F81 target, but zero source-authorized
elementwise assignments. The contact lane is not executed. This prevents
outcome fitting from filling the missing join and keeps computation pruning
from rewriting the physical source surfaces.

## 5. Carrier and container return chain

### 5.1 QP:QP092d conservation premise

QP:QP092d evaluates seven particle rows, six macro rows and 18 propagation
rows. All rows pass its conservation checks. Across three ledger-closure
scopes it records

\[
\frac18q_A+\frac78q_A=q_A
\]

with maximum relative error

\[
2.98274132099\times10^{-19}.
\]

It adds zero matter rows, rejects direct \(q_A\)-as-mass on 24 of 24 rows,
and rejects all six wrong controls. Its mechanism status retains source
support through tensor carrier and ledger compression.

### 5.2 QP:QP101@SAM-ARCHIVE carrier inventory audit

The archived QP101 audit records a 299-row V2 catalog and 13 added rows. Five
named carrier roles occur at values

\[
1,\ 8,\ 9,\ 18,\ 81,
\]

and eight lifted connector rows occur at

\[
1,\ 2,\ 3,\ 4,\ 6,\ 8,\ 9,\ 12.
\]

The forbidden \(\Theta18\) retained-lift value is

\[
\frac{81}{4}=20.25,
\]

and the audit records no violations. The result supports the heterogeneous
role inventory in SAMA-C000136-R001. It does not place the separate A-field
carrier inside the thirteen-value full-face expression.

### 5.3 QP:QP112@SAM-ARCHIVE A/Theta correspondence

The archived QP112 audit records 114 substrate-closed entries, 42 matter rows
with partners, 30 without partners and 42 antimatter rows. Its scale
correspondence places the A route:

\[
\begin{array}{ll}
d=0:& A\text{ at }R=12\text{ inside the }\Theta\text{ surface},\\
d=1:& A\text{ at }R^2=144\text{ meeting the }\Theta\text{ surface},\\
d=2:& A\text{ at }R^3=1728\text{ outside the }\Theta\text{ surface}.
\end{array}
\]

All 16 declared \(d=1\) antimatter matches are retained. The correspondence
connects scales; it does not identify A-conjugation, \(\Theta\) traffic,
X1/W8/W9 or matter rows as one type.

### 5.4 CR:CR281@09a functional classifier

For an exact address

\[
\mathcal A=h^a d^b,
\]

define

\[
F_{cc}(\mathcal A)=
\begin{cases}
\mathrm{TERMINAL\ CLOSURE},
&\mathcal A=\mathcal L=162,\\
\mathrm{FIXED\ POINT},
&(a,b)=(2,1)\text{ or }a=0,\ b\ge3,\\
\mathrm{OUTPUT},
&a=1,\ 1\le b\le3\text{ or }(a,b)=(0,2),\\
\mathrm{OUT},
&\text{otherwise}.
\end{cases}
\]

Substitution gives:

\[
\begin{array}{ccl}
6=h^1d^1&\mapsto&\mathrm{OUTPUT},\\
9=d^2&\mapsto&\mathrm{OUTPUT},\\
18=h^1d^2&\mapsto&\mathrm{OUTPUT},\\
54=h^1d^3&\mapsto&\mathrm{OUTPUT},\\
12=h^2d^1&\mapsto&\mathrm{FIXED\ POINT},\\
27=d^3&\mapsto&\mathrm{FIXED\ POINT},\\
81=d^4&\mapsto&\mathrm{FIXED\ POINT},\\
162=hd^4&\mapsto&\mathrm{TERMINAL\ CLOSURE}.
\end{array}
\]

The held-out structural values retain their own roles:
\(M=126\) is retained scratch, \(R^2=144\) is domain total, \(S=8\) is domain
multiplicity, and \(h^4=16\) is outside the source atom set.

### 5.5 CR:CR282@09a registered lineage and exact boundary

The permanent key CR:CR282@09a refers to the A-operator row-trace,
axis-self-closure parent. It retains:

- the historical row trace;
- retirement of the duplicate A-field-carrier row;
- the 162-versus-163 ledger control;
- non-row A-conjugation; and
- an open direct A-to-axis-self weld.

A separate CR282 appeal artifact is source evidence for the narrower bridge

\[
B/\mathrm{contact}
\longrightarrow
X1\text{ axis fee}
\longrightarrow
W9.
\]

That separate appeal has no permanent exact test key in the current registry,
so this document does not invent one. Its source-bound statement is carried by

\[
A\longrightarrow B/\mathrm{contact}\quad\text{open}
\]

and

\[
A\longrightarrow X1\quad\text{open}.
\]

### 5.6 CR:CR283@09a cardinality contact and failed geometry

The stable surface has

\[
P=80=48\text{ matter}+32\text{ antimatter}
\]

and also

\[
P=64\text{ charged}+16\text{ neutral}.
\]

From

\[
W9=8+1=9
\]

derive

\[
F=W9^2=9^2=81.
\]

Then

\[
\begin{aligned}
P
&=F-1\\
&=81-1\\
&=(9-1)(9+1)\\
&=8\cdot10\\
&=80.
\end{aligned}
\]

The additional unit is a non-row closure/contact address:

\[
80\text{ active rows}
+1\text{ closure address}
=81\text{ face cardinality}.
\]

The terminal ledger is

\[
\mathcal L=2F=2\cdot81=162.
\]

The arithmetic contact and row typing are retained. The punctured-grid and
ten-octet searches supply the preserved negative evidence for
SAMA-C000183-R001: no specific row-to-face geometry is selected.

### 5.7 G:G1@SAM-RESEARCH computation boundary

The G1 audit keeps three domains separate:

\[
100\text{ source-native N100 rows},
\]

\[
72\text{ Shared72 aggregate-incidence-eligible rows},
\]

\[
81\text{ F81 semantic sites with ten contact classes}.
\]

The missing object is one source-authorized elementwise relation from a QP
candidate or template to an F81 site or address class. Because no such key is
present, the audit retains

\[
\text{contact lane status}
=\mathrm{NOT\ EXECUTED}.
\]

This is the correct serialization boundary, not a request to infer a map from
an outcome.

## 6. Matter-return replay

### 6.1 LC:LC03 source anchor

LC:LC03 replays

\[
\text{closed matter}
\to q_A
\to \frac18\text{ carrier}
\to \text{compression}
\to A(r)
\to \text{readout}.
\]

It retains

\[
\text{carrier side}=\frac18,\qquad
\text{retained side}=\frac78,
\]

\[
R^2 2^{-D}=144\cdot2^{-3}=18,
\]

\[
\alpha_H D^2=2\cdot3^2=18,
\]

and distinguishes the surface debit

\[
\frac{D^2}{R}=\frac9{12}=\frac34
\]

from the carrier fraction \(1/8\). Fifteen wrong controls are rejected,
including direct \(q_A\)-as-mass, missing compression, missing carrier,
incorrect fractions, carrier promotion, lane mixing and treating \(A=1\) as
a normal launch point.

### 6.2 LC:LC06 null-witness retest

LC:LC06 returns to the matter admission boundary. Its 126-row inventory is

\[
63\text{ single writes}+63\text{ bound composites},
\]

with neither antimatter rows nor carrier promotion. The null-conjugate witness
has

\[
\text{native value}=18,\qquad \text{debit}=18
\]

but

\[
M_{\rm obs}=0,\qquad q_A=0.
\]

The retest therefore joins the beginning and end of the route: a carrier
number can be present in the grammar while the matter/source outputs remain
zero.

## 7. A-conjugation boundary

### 7.1 Neutral rows expose the carrier fraction directly

For each of the 16 neutral matter rows, the compact source law is

\[
q_A(p,d)=\frac{p}{8}R^d.
\]

The coefficient is not an empirical decimal. It is the same exact structural
fraction seen in the closed-domain and source-support splits:

\[
\frac18=2^{-D}=\frac{\Theta}{R^2}.
\]

Thus a neutral matter row can emit source support through the carrier fraction
without acquiring a charged conjugate. The focused evidence remains routed
through SAMA-D000028; this chapter receives the exact law through
SAMA-C000153-R001 and its prerequisite handoff.

### 7.2 Charged A-conjugation

CR:CR256@09a defines the non-row A operator for charged antecedents:

\[
q_{A,\bar m}=q_{A,m}C_A(s,p,d).
\]

For the negative branch,

\[
C_A(-)=\frac56
\left(1+\frac{p}{R^{d+1}}\right),
\]

while for the positive branch,

\[
C_A(+)=\frac65
\left(1-\frac{p}{R^{d+1}}\right).
\]

At the positive \(p=12,d=0\) address,

\[
\frac{p}{R^{d+1}}
=\frac{12}{12}=1,
\]

so

\[
C_A(+)
=\frac65(1-1)
=0.
\]

Therefore

\[
q_{A,\bar m}=0
\]

for that declared hard-zero case. The operator acts on 32 charged matter
antecedents and emits conjugate rows; the operator itself never increments the
census.

### 7.3 Notational firewall

Three uses of the letter A must remain disjoint:

\[
\begin{array}{ll}
A=Z+N,&\text{nucleon count},\\
A(r)=r_s/r,&\text{accumulation field},\\
\mathsf A,&\text{non-row conjugation operator}.
\end{array}
\]

This notational firewall prevents the scale correspondence in QP112 from
becoming an invalid type identity.

## 8. Composite return and the full-face ledger

The owner-supplied ordered role-value expression is

\[
1,1,2,3,4,6,8,8,9,9,12,18,81.
\]

The first twelve values sum to

\[
1+1+2+3+4+6+8+8+9+9+12+18=81.
\]

Adding the final full-face value gives

\[
81+81=162.
\]

The thirteen positions are heterogeneous. They include X1/W8/W9 relational
roles, mass-lift values, \(\Theta18\) carrier traffic and the F81 aggregate.
The separate A-field carrier is transport infrastructure outside this
expression. SAMA-C000135-R001 and SAMA-C000136-R001 preserve both the exact
arithmetic and the role firewall.

For a closed composite, the return sequence is:

\[
\begin{aligned}
\text{constituent rows}
&\longrightarrow \text{connector/debit configuration}\\
&\longrightarrow \text{composite closure}\\
&\longrightarrow q_A\\
&\longrightarrow (T,W)\\
&\longrightarrow F_{cc}\text{ address role}\\
&\longrightarrow \mathcal C_{\rm ledger}\\
&\longrightarrow A(r).
\end{aligned}
\]

The connected configuration is not discarded when its scalar support is
compressed. It remains the provenance of the emitted source.

## 9. Typed Volume III serialization handoff

Volume III receives separate fields for:

- connector value and role;
- carrier value, release fraction and lift status;
- matter-row source ID, address and support channels;
- antimatter antecedent, A-branch and hard-zero status;
- non-row operator identity;
- composite connector/debit configuration;
- nucleus \((Z,N,A)\) and its source channels;
- element/isotope/representative type;
- closure/contact address;
- compressed A-source output; and
- exact qualified evidence keys.

Exact rationals remain exact:

\[
\frac18,\quad
\frac5{48},\quad
\frac{17}{192},\quad
\frac{7117}{768},\quad
\frac{7093}{192}.
\]

Decimal projections may be emitted for display, but they do not replace the
rational field. SAMA-C000184-R001 owns this interface.

The computation firewall is

\[
\text{physical source census}
\ne
\text{computation grammar census}.
\]

An N100 program can consume a 126-row periodic table or a 321-row catalog
without changing either source table. G:G1@SAM-RESEARCH retains the missing
elementwise N100-to-F81 join as an exact input dependency.

## 10. Established result, exact open relations and forward handoff

Within the registered source scope, the chain establishes:

1. Closed matter remains matter while emitting \(q_A\) source support.
2. \(q_A\) decomposes exactly into \(q_A/8\) carrier and \(7q_A/8\)
   retained support.
3. Compression is required before accumulation-field readout.
4. \(\Theta18\) is output traffic with zero rest closure, not a fixed
   container or matter row.
5. CR:CR281@09a classifies output, fixed and terminal addresses from exact
   \(h^a d^b\) structure.
6. A-conjugation is a non-row transform and retains its hard zero.
7. The active ledger remains 162; restoring the retired duplicate row gives
   the rejected 163 control.
8. The supported contact route is
   \(B/\mathrm{contact}\to X1\text{ axis fee}\to W9\).
9. \(80+1=81\) and \(2F=162\) retain a cardinality and typed-ledger contact.
10. Volume III receives types and exact arithmetic rather than a flattened
    number list.

The exact open relations are:

- \(A\to B/\mathrm{contact}\);
- direct \(A\to X1\);
- a specific row-to-face geometry for the \(80+1=81\) contact;
- the source-authorized elementwise N100-to-F81 join;
- a complete nonlinear composite-to-chemistry operator; and
- any present ATOM3D or binding continuation not installed by its live
  authority.

Every crosswalk row assigned to this document has
\(result\_classification=\mathrm{null}\). No source status or verdict is
rewritten.

The return completes Volume II's conceptual loop:

\[
\text{substrate supports finite matter}
\longrightarrow
\text{matter closes}
\longrightarrow
\text{matter emits typed support}
\longrightarrow
\text{support returns through substrate relations}.
\]

SAMA-P000003 assembles that loop from the full child-document chain.
SAMA-P000006 continues the finite-particle, binding and computation route
without replacing the open welds.

# Theta18 Carrier and Tensor Return

## ATOM3D: contact, grammar and signed decoding — 14 September 2026

A3D41-T18-CONTACT-R2 uses SLC-GEN3-R3; A3D41-RXT-R3 supplies the joint native successor. Ordinary contact retains four minima and 113,664 agreeing readouts. Li-6 grammar retains both selected covers and every tie across six placements and 128 rho settings. Two Write responses and a signed N01 bit recover all 192 tested configurations. The test result suggests strong contact with the concept. The separate distinct-selector and physical-coefficient/MeV work remain owner-paused.

[Current derivations, code and results](../../research/atom3d/README.md).

## Retained source-era derivation and results

The following development retains its original experimental context and revision fields. Historical engine selections and campaign status in this source-era account are superseded by the dated current section above.


## Conceptual abstract

Theta18 is the first overflow and the principal tensor-carrier candidate in
the tested SAM grammar. It is the released one-eighth lane of the 144 contact
budget, the 18-slot overlap in the two-sided 81/81 closure construction, the
difference between 144 and 126, the difference between 162 and 144, and the
normalization quantum that sends the retained/native/full capacities to the
ordered ladder 7|8|9.

These equalities describe one repeated carrier role, but they do not erase
type. Theta18 is not X1, matter, rest mass, the inactive A-field row proxy or
an ordinary p12 fee. Its directional address has 262,144 states,

\[
2^{18}=512\cdot512=4^9=|\mathbb Z_4^9|,
\]

with the two 512 views coupled by modulo-four carry. The factorization is an
address identity, not two independent 512-case machines.

The return route begins from M126 retention. A fee-exempt Theta18 lift yields
the independent N144 state, and same/reversed L162 images place those
`2^144` states inside a constrained `2^162` ambient occurrence surface.
L162 is therefore a readout ceiling, not 162 independent spins. The complete
chain preserves carrier, state, sidecar and readout as different objects.

## 1. Opening question and conceptual picture

The central question is:

> What carries a completed finite matter write back into the directional
> computation surface, and how does that carrier enlarge state without being
> counted as matter or folded into the X1 response?

The source-bound answer is a typed route:

```text
two N72 custody packets
  -> M126 retained matter/output state
  -- independent fee-exempt Theta18 lift -->
N144 independent union state
  -- same/reversed occurrence imaging and typed mismatch sidecar -->
N144, N144+d or L162 readout
```

The carrier is active in the transition while remaining distinct from both
the retained matter state and the response interface. D39 supplies X1 as the
transient response channel. This document assigns the tested moving-carrier
burden to Theta18.

## 2. Exact derivation of the carrier constants

### 2.1 From the SAM primitives to Theta18

Start with the registered substrate primitives

\[
\hat h=2,
\qquad D=3,
\qquad R=\hat h^2D=12,
\]

and the substrate and resolved-state values

\[
S=\hat h^D=8,
\qquad W=D^2=9.
\]

Theta follows in two exact ways:

\[
\Theta=\hat hW=2\cdot9=18,
\]

and

\[
\Theta=\frac{R^2}{S}=\frac{12^2}{8}=\frac{144}{8}=18.
\]

The agreement is not a new type assignment. It shows that the same 18-valued
carrier can be read from the resolved-state route and from the one-eighth
partition of the 144 contact domain.

### 2.2 Retained, native and full capacities

Let

\[
N=R^2=144.
\]

Removing the one-eighth carrier lane gives retained matter/output capacity

\[
M=N-\Theta=144-18=126=7\Theta.
\]

Adding the same carrier lane above N gives the full constrained readout

\[
L=N+\Theta=144+18=162=9\Theta.
\]

The complete normalized ladder is then

\[
\frac{M}{\Theta}=\frac{126}{18}=7,
\qquad
\frac{N}{\Theta}=\frac{144}{18}=8,
\qquad
\frac{L}{\Theta}=\frac{162}{18}=9,
\]

with the release check

\[
\frac{\Theta}{\Theta}=1.
\]

The numerical sequence aligns with retained seven-eighths, unresolved S8,
resolved W9 and the normalized X1 scalar occurrence. The alignment does not
make Theta18 identical to X1: Theta is the carrier quantum used in the
normalization; X1 remains the response channel in the lifecycle.

### 2.3 The coupled directional address

Theta18 also fixes the exact directional address size:

\[
2^{18}=262{,}144.
\]

Because

\[
262{,}144=512^2=(2^9)^2=2^{18}=4^9,
\]

the same address surface can be encoded as two 512-sided views or as nine
modulo-four coordinates:

\[
\mathbb Z_4^9.
\]

The equality is operational only with carry preserved. Signed translation in
one coordinate can propagate through the packed modulo-four representation;
the two 512 projections therefore cannot be executed as unrelated factors and
recombined afterward. The historical directional control reports that such a
split changes 1,179,648 direction/record cases. The correct object is one
coupled 18-bit address.

## 3. Type system for Theta return

### 3.1 Carrier-only and fee-exempt

Theta18 has the following positive type:

| Field | Theta18 assignment |
|---|---|
| role | first overflow / released tensor carrier / overlap |
| value | 18 |
| lift fee | zero in the retained computation |
| address | coupled 18-bit `Z4^9` directional surface |
| matter ownership | none |
| rest-mass lift | none |
| relation to X1 | distinct carrier supporting a lifecycle whose response channel is X1 |

Ordinary retained connector or matter roles can receive lift or surface
credit. Theta18 does not. In particular, an ordinary p12 route can carry a
fee/value convention of 13 in its own adapter, while Theta18 remains value 18
with fee zero. The historical charged value `81/4` is a diagnostic consequence
of assigning the wrong fee to the carrier; it is not the Theta18 return used
here.

### 3.2 State, fiber, sidecar and readout

| Object | Independent rank or address size | Type |
|---|---:|---|
| M126 | 126 | retained matter/output state |
| Theta18 | 18 | independent carrier/fiber lift |
| N144 | 144 | independent union state |
| mismatch sidecar `d` | 0 through 18 under the declared fixture | active defect coordinates, separately typed |
| L162 | ambient 162 | constrained occurrence image/readout ceiling |

Only M126 plus the independent Theta18 lift produces the `2^144` N144 state
count. Adding a mismatch sidecar changes the application route to `N144+d`;
it does not retroactively change the independent rank of every L162 image.

### 3.3 Carrier versus response

D39 establishes the lifecycle

\[
\operatorname{RESOLVE}(S8,B,X1)\to W9.
\]

Theta18 enters alongside that relation through the B partition:

\[
144\to(126\text{ retained},18\text{ released}).
\]

The roles are therefore complementary:

- Theta18 supplies the released carrier quantum and directional address;
- X1 supplies the transient axis/response occurrence;
- W9 records the resolved composite relation; and
- M126 retains the matter/output state.

The exact scalar relation `18/18=1` is a normalization readout. It does not
authorize `Theta18=X1`.

## 4. M126 to N144 to L162, step by step

### 4.1 Retain two N72 custody packets as M126

The structured application begins with two exact N72 custody packets. Their
full source information is not treated as 144 unconstrained matter variables.
The declared retention operator produces M126: 126 independent retained
matter/output coordinates. The missing 18 coordinates are not lost data; they
are carried as the separately typed released Theta lane.

At the state-count level, the retained state contains

\[
2^{126}
\]

configurations.

### 4.2 Apply the independent carrier lift

The fee-exempt carrier has `2^18` directional addresses. Taking the independent
product yields

\[
2^{126}\cdot2^{18}=2^{144}.
\]

This is the N144 union state. The product is valid because the carrier is an
independent lift at this seam. It would be invalid to fold the multiplicity
into X1 or count Theta18 as 18 more matter rows.

### 4.3 Form same and reversed L162 images

The application then constructs two occurrence views—same and reversed—inside
an ambient 162-coordinate ledger. Both views retain exactly the `2^144` valid
N144 states. Thus

\[
|\operatorname{image}_{same}|=|\operatorname{image}_{reversed}|=2^{144},
\]

even though the formal ambient ceiling is

\[
2^{162}.
\]

This is the difference between readout arity and independent rank. The L162
slots can record where an occurrence appears; they are not 162 freely
selectable physical spins.

### 4.4 Route the mismatch sidecar

For a declared fixture, define

\[
d_t=\operatorname{popcount}(\text{same-mismatch mask}).
\]

The phase-aware application route is

```text
d=0       -> N144
0<d<18    -> N144+d
d=18      -> L162
```

The route preserves the state/readout distinction:

1. no mismatch means the N144 state is already sufficient;
2. a partial mismatch appends only the active defect coordinates;
3. a complete 18-coordinate mismatch reaches the L162 occurrence view.

Held-out fixtures receive exact extension rather than truncation. The sidecar
is application data, not a new global SLC state-space definition.

## 5. Worked source examples

### 5.1 The Theta-normalized 7|8|9 construction

CR120X begins at route depth `d=1`:

\[
R^{d+1}=R^2=144.
\]

B/contact partitions that domain into

\[
144=126+18.
\]

Dividing each load-bearing capacity by the registered carrier gives

\[
126\mapsto7,
\qquad144\mapsto8,
\qquad162\mapsto9,
\qquad18\mapsto1.
\]

All current registered carrier candidates were substituted with both native
payload and partition scalar. QP093A-0300 Theta18 was the sole carrier that
reproduced the entire ladder in both comparisons. In the frozen depth
comparison, only `d=1` returned `R^(d+1)=144=S8*Theta`; all 32 CR256 rows
replayed to maximum reported residual `5.000e-08`.

### 5.2 Higgs reveal arithmetic inside CR223f

CR223f includes a cross-domain tensor-carrier discriminant whose registered
Higgs candidate begins only from R and D:

\[
H_{reveal}
=R^2(1-2^{-D})-\frac{D^2}{R}.
\]

Substituting `R=12` and `D=3` gives

\[
H_{reveal}
=144\left(1-\frac18\right)-\frac9{12}
=144\cdot\frac78-\frac34
=126-0.75
=125.25.
\]

The source result reports this against the PDG 2024 value
`125.25 ± 0.16 GeV`. CR223f also registers a 126-element classification
candidate and a same-platform PR-qutrit firing coefficient, while rejecting
the renamed decoherence-asymmetry example at its entry gate. Fourteen of
fourteen checks pass; three of four candidates qualify.

Its exact source result class is
`CR223f_CP_INDEPENDENT_SIGNATURE_SUPPORTED__CROSS_DOMAIN_MATCHED__SAME_DOMAIN_AWAITING_CR223c`.
The collider and nuclear matches are cross-domain relative to the PR-qutrit
question, and the same-domain measurement remains pending. This document uses
CR223f as a registered carrier-discriminant premise, not as a universal
physical-identity assignment for Theta18.

### 5.3 Model-wide stress rather than one identity

CR120Y retests Theta18 through six exact derivation families. The candidate
must also survive each leave-one-family-out fold, the full structural-output
family `{6,9,18,54}`, route-depth alternatives, QP carrier alternatives,
96 Starbreaker scenarios and twelve wrong controls.

The result retains Theta18 in all six folds, selects `d=1` only, selects
QP093A-0300 only in both frozen value modes, and preserves the
`18/126/18` ledger typing in all 96 Starbreaker scenarios. The alternatives
6, 9 and 54 remain valid in their own registered roles; they fail only the
specific primary-carrier task.

## 6. Deviation, correction and retest chain

### 6.1 Deviation inherited from D39

The carrier result is intelligible only with the earlier failures present.
CR120V rejected the literal assignment of scalar halves to B and X1. CR120W
then found one typed A-field-carrier candidate but failed the full executable
weld and one of twelve wrong controls. Neither result identifies the moving
carrier.

### 6.2 Corrected question

CR120X asks which registered carrier normalizes the existing ledger
capacities, rather than asking a fixed runtime to expose a nonexistent X1
intervention. This is a change of executable question, not a deletion of the
failed artifacts. It produces the carrier/response separation:

```text
Theta18: released carrier quantum
X1:      normalized response occurrence
```

### 6.3 Retest

CR120Y then removes each evidence family in turn and preserves all wrong
controls. Its exact source verdict retains Theta18 as the primary carrier
candidate with the physical-identity boundary intact. The corrected route
supplements, rather than overwrites, the failed literal-half and direct-weld
routes.

## 7. Controls and why each changes the object

| Control | What it tests | Why the wrong version is different |
|---|---|---|
| split `512×512` | coupled address covariance | independent factors remove modulo-four carry and change directional records |
| assign ordinary p12 fee | carrier fee status | adding retained lift/surface credit turns fee-exempt Theta traffic into another role |
| identify Theta with X1 | carrier/response type firewall | a normalization ratio is not entity identity |
| restore A-field row | non-row operator custody | ledger becomes 163 rather than canonical 162 |
| use L162 as 162 spins | readout/rank firewall | replaces `2^144` valid images with an untested `2^162` independent state space |
| ignore `d` sidecar | fixture-dependent defect custody | discards the coordinates that select N144, N144+d or L162 |
| choose carrier after one ratio | candidate-selection control | model-wide substitution and leave-one-family-out stress are bypassed |
| treat cross-domain CR223f as same-platform closure | comparator-domain boundary | the registered result explicitly retains same-domain CR223c as pending |

The controls are constructive. They show which algebraic equalities survive
the carrier type and which equalities merely arise after changing the tested
object.

## 8. C4 state return, retained history and re-emission

The current moving-information continuation makes Theta18 operational as an
ordered C4 history surface without changing its carrier-only, fee-exempt type.
The orbit-wave identity

\[
J_\Theta^*G_\Theta J_\Theta=L_{C4}
\]

joins returning state to retained and outgoing history. `P_C4^4=I`, but the
cumulative wave action has positive derivative: the state returns while its
history continues.

Starbreaker E/M1 compiles all 17,922 active Theta18 packets into 384/384
returning elliptic two-body relations. J1 carries those relations into live
ATOM3D receiver Grams. J2 then derives three disjoint output cycles directly
from off-fibre signed reciprocal holonomy,

\[
a_{j,k}=h_j+k e_j,
\qquad k=0,1,2,3,
\]

and emits 1,536 ordered generation-two phase packets. No digest or target
reveal selects an address. Endpoint collapse erases both ordered and symmetric
content; C2 collapse retains only one response mode. These controls locate the
full C4 order as load-bearing.

The orbit-wave, E/M1 and J2 results each suggest strong contact with the
concept. They remain isolated/non-promoted and assign no new physical carrier
identity or scale.

## 9. Established result and current boundaries

The source-bound state at this document is:

1. Theta18 is exactly `18=hW=R^2/S=N-M=L-N`.
2. It is the tested one-eighth released carrier and a coupled 18-bit
   `Z4^9` directional address.
3. It is fee-exempt and has no ordinary matter/rest-mass lift.
4. It is distinct from X1, the A-field carrier, the historical A-row proxy
   and ordinary p12 roles.
5. Its independent lift sends M126 state count to N144:
   `2^126*2^18=2^144`.
6. L162 is a constrained occurrence readout whose same/reversed images retain
   `2^144` valid states; it is not 162 independent spins.
7. CR120X supplies the exact 7|8|9 discovery route, and CR120Y retains the
   candidate through model-wide stress.
8. CR223f supplies a registered cross-domain carrier discriminant with its
   same-domain boundary still explicit.
9. Physical carrier identity, numerical energy calibration, the direct
   A-to-X1 weld and universal application routing remain outside this result.
10. The historical spine labels do not replace the live `SLCQ2-RZ` sole
    current/default identity.

No atomic source record assigned to D40 carries an authorized project result
classification. This revision preserves the registered source verdicts and
does not create one.

# ATOM3D Theta Matter Reception and Re-emission

## ATOM3D: contact, grammar and signed decoding — 14 September 2026

A3D41-T18-CONTACT-R2 uses SLC-GEN3-R3; A3D41-RXT-R3 supplies the joint native successor. Ordinary contact retains four minima and 113,664 agreeing readouts. Li-6 grammar retains both selected covers and every tie across six placements and 128 rho settings. Two Write responses and a signed N01 bit recover all 192 tested configurations. The test result suggests strong contact with the concept. The separate distinct-selector and physical-coefficient/MeV work remain owner-paused.

[Current derivations, code and results](../../research/atom3d/README.md).

## Retained source-era derivation and results

The following development retains its original experimental context and revision fields. Historical engine selections and campaign status in this source-era account are superseded by the dated current section above.


## Conceptual abstract

The current ATOM3D continuation asks what happens after signed moving
information reaches matter. It does not treat a returned local state as an
erasure of the path that produced it. The receiver retains relation identity,
forward and reverse signed frames, the response Gram and continuous custody.
Those retained objects first select one Li-6 receiver class, then support a
live Starbreaker-to-ATOM3D causal chain, and finally generate new ordered
Theta18 traffic and an informational response geometry.

The executed route is

```text
TDI3 pair ordinal + signed Starbreaker q-incidence
  -> one source-native Li-6 relation/frame class
  -> 384 positive-definite reciprocal receiver responses
  -> live J1 source/emission/receiver custody
  -> off-fibre reciprocal holonomy
  -> three disjoint C4 Theta18 output cycles
  -> 1,536 generation-two phase packets
  -> all-nine signed matter innovation Delta(rho)
  -> rank-four informational response geometry
  -> typed RH event-placement breadcrumb.
```

Every successor is isolated and non-promoted. ATOM3D's sole current/default
base remains `SLCQ2-RZ-A3D-SR3`; the newer Q3/CEV1 work changes no pointer and
assigns no physical mass, energy, distance, duration or detector scale.

## 1. TMR1 source-native receiver selection

### 1.1 Executable question

TMR1 asks whether TDI3 pair ordinals and the actual signed Starbreaker
q-incidences select a unique member of the three AF2-open Li-6 relation
families without target labels or fitted scores.

The candidate space begins with 13,824 combinations. Source-visible gates
reduce it exactly:

```text
13,824 raw candidates
   -> 64 incidence-compatible candidates
   -> 8 reciprocal-frame candidates
   -> 1 selected receiver class.
```

The selected forward frames are

```text
AF_0_2_Z-
AF_2_3_Y+
AF_4_5_Y-
```

with their reciprocal X frames retained separately. This is a relation and
frame selection, not a relabelling of the Q3 reciprocal scorer.

### 1.2 Receiver response

Each of the 384 E/M1 relation bundles enters in forward and reverse channels.
After exact state return, the forward, reverse and combined response Grams are
positive definite for all `384/384` bundles. Unit, primary and corrected
producer-independent checks pass `10/10`, `11/11` and `11/11`.

The first independent attempt is preserved: it serialized a canonical tuple
as a JSON list and compared representation types rather than their exact
semantic identity. The corrected reconstruction compares canonical content
and passes without changing the mathematical result.

**The test result suggests strong contact with the concept.**

## 2. J1 live Starbreaker-to-ATOM3D execution

### 2.1 One causal execution rather than artifact replay

J1 runs the source and receiver in the same bounded execution. Its exact
ledger predecessor chain is

```text
19,184 source contacts
     6 ray-cycle ledgers
   384 Starbreaker E/M1 emission ledgers
   384 ATOM3D receiver ledgers
-------
19,958 exact custody ledgers.
```

The terminal DAG closes 17,922 active packets, all six character cycles, all
384 emissions, all 384 receivers and 768 cross-domain edges. The receiver is
therefore causally joined to the live emission multiset rather than loaded
from a prior result file.

### 2.2 Preserved implementation faults and corrected validation

The first unit launch used a fixture named `run`, shadowing
`unittest.TestCase.run` before its assertions executed. The corrected suite
passes `13/13`. The first completed independent validation then compared
JSON-loaded moment-cycle lists with canonically equal reconstructed tuples.
That representation-only mismatch is preserved; corrected semantic comparison
passes `14/14`, matching the `14/14` primary execution.

These corrections supplement the record. They do not erase the first attempts
or change the executable mathematical question.

**The test result suggests strong contact with the concept.**

## 3. J2 source-native Theta18 re-emission

### 3.1 Off-fibre history supplies the address

For relation coordinate `q_j`, let `v_+` and `v_-` be the full forward and
reverse signed-ray translations in `Z4^9`. Their local entries cancel, while
the other coordinates retain composition history:

\[
h_j=v_++v_-\pmod 4,
\qquad (h_j)_j=0.
\]

The forward local fibre supplies the ordered C4 output cycle

\[
a_{j,k}=h_j+k e_j,
\qquad k=0,1,2,3.
\]

The three receiver relations generate three pairwise disjoint cycles with 12
nonzero addresses, none present in J1's 3,053-address incoming support. No
digest, target reveal or fitted selector enters the address law.

### 3.2 Response payload and custody

For a positive receiver Gram

\[
G=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\b&c\end{pmatrix},
\]

the antipodal carrier matrix

\[
A=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\0&1\\-1&0\\0&-1\end{pmatrix}
\]

gives the integral phase lift `K_G=A G A^T`. It is positive semidefinite of
rank two, has zero row sums, is killed by the C2 projection and pulls back to
the receiver:

\[
(A/2)^T K_G(A/2)=G.
\]

All 384 receiver Grams pass lift, pullback, C4 twirl and controls. They emit
1,536 ordered generation-two packets. Three basis events and 384 re-emission
events add 387 custody rows, extending `19,958 -> 20,345` without erasure.

## 4. All-nine signed matter innovation

J2 also applies signed ray action before the Gram across every TDI3 relation.
With incidence `B_(v,e)` and separate forward/reverse amplitudes,

\[
A_{v,d}=\sum_e B_{v,e}X_{e,d},
\qquad
G_v=A_{v,F}^T A_{v,F}+A_{v,R}^T A_{v,R}.
\]

Subtracting the diagonal self contribution from the coherent six-site sum
gives

\[
\Delta(\rho)=A\rho^2+B\rho+C.
\]

Exact completion of the square records positive-definite leading and Schur
matrices, hence

\[
\Delta(\rho)>0
\]

for every real `rho`, not only the 128 executed integer supports. The roster
contains 1,152 signed relation amplitudes, 768 site-response rows, 2,560 NZA1
endpoint interactions, 128 global innovations and 128 response geometries.

**The test result suggests strong contact with the concept.**

## 5. Controls locate the two-mode structure

| Control | Outcome | What it locates |
|---|---|---|
| endpoint-only return | `Delta=0` | retained off-fibre history is load-bearing |
| remove ray signs | `Delta(1)` becomes indefinite | signed reciprocal orientation is load-bearing |
| C2 collapse | positive rank-one first mode; second erased | full C4 order carries the second mode |
| delete one relation | every deletion changes the operator | no relation is an inert decoration |
| inspect N02 | dark at all 128 supports | EDGE006 and EDGE084 cancel locally while their q4/q7 histories remain distinct |

The six 16-coordinate response feature rows sum to zero. Their exact response
Gram has rank four at every support; the three-largest-feature projection has
rank three, and the response-distance automorphism group contains only the
identity. This is an informational AFC3-compatible geometry update without a
physical scale assignment.

## 6. RH breadcrumb and exact boundary

The J2 innovation can enter the H000723 threshold cell first at the compatible
third-jet location

\[
\beta_k'(D)\Delta(\rho).
\]

This is a typed source-side candidate contribution. The actual prime-power
source map, event weight, placement in the completed current and simultaneous
product normal remain open. No RH classification is inferred from the matter
result alone.

## 7. Result and evidence index

| Stage | Evidence route | Checks / result |
|---|---|---|
| TMR1 | `SLC/18_SAM_NATIVE_QC/SLCQ3_RZ_CEV1_A3D_TMR1/release/EXPLORATION_RESULT.md` | `10/10`, `11/11`, `11/11`; strong contact |
| J1 | `SLC/18_SAM_NATIVE_QC/SLCQ3_RZ_CEV1_SB_A3D_J1/release/EXPLORATION_RESULT.md` | `13/13`, `14/14`, `14/14`; strong contact |
| J2 | `SLC/18_SAM_NATIVE_QC/SLCQ3_RZ_CEV1_SB_A3D_J2/release/EXPLORATION_RESULT.md` | `14/14`, `18/18`, `28/28`; strong contact |

These frozen campaign paths are source evidence routes. They are not assigned
new legacy G/QP/CR/LC keys.

## 8. Atomic-record index

| `SAMA-C000268-R001` | TMR1 source-native Li-6 receiver selection and response |

## 9. Revision and approval boundary

This is unapproved `SAMA-D000058`, revision 1. Mechanical validation and the

TMR1, J1 and J2 remain isolated/non-promoted; every current SLC, RH,
